    • \n\t

\n\n

\n", "comp": "", "opts": ["DBCA", "ABDC", "BCDA", "BADC"], "cidx": 3, "sol": "

The correct answer is 'BADC'.

\n\n

Key Points

\n\n
    \n\t
  • The logical sequence of the given question is as follows:\n\t
      \n\t\t
    • B. The Indian union cabinet approved the country's national education strategy on 29 July 2020.\n\n\t\t
        \n\t\t\t
      • This line introduces the inception of the national education strategy in India, signifying an important event where the strategy was officially sanctioned.
        • \n\t\t
      \n\t\t
      • \n\t\t
    • A. It superseded India's previous educational strategy, which was created in 1986.\n\t\t
        \n\t\t\t
      • Here, it's explained that the newly approved strategy replaced the old one from 1986, indicating a shift or upgrade in the educational approach taken by the country.
        • \n\t\t
      \n\t\t
      • \n\t\t
    • D. This policy significantly improves India's educational system. Restructuring India's educational system is the primary goal of the National Education Policy 2022.\n\t\t
        \n\t\t\t
      • This line elaborates on the impact and goal of the new strategy, emphasizing its purpose of reforming and enhancing the educational system in India.
        • \n\t\t
      \n\t\t
      • \n\t\t
    • C. And it provides a framework for education from the primary grades up to higher education, as well as for vocational training in both urban and rural settings.\n\t\t
        \n\t\t\t
      • Lastly, this sentence outlines the comprehensive nature of the strategy, indicating that it covers education from primary levels through higher education, along with vocational training in diverse settings, thus completing the contextual picture of the national education strategy.
        • \n\t\t
      \n\t\t
      • \n\t
    \n\t
    • \n\t
  • Thus, the correct answer is Option 4.
    \n\t 
    • \n
\n\n

Correct Sequence: The Indian union cabinet approved the country's national education strategy on 29 July 2020.  It superseded India's previous educational strategy, which was created in 1986. This policy significantly improves India's educational system. Restructuring India's educational system is the primary goal of the National Education Policy 2022.  And it provides a framework for education from the primary grades up to higher education, as well as for vocational training in both urban and rural settings.

\n\n

Important Points

\n\n
    \n\t
  • Practice is key! Regularly practicing rearranging sentences or paragraphs will improve your ability to discern logical sequences and enhance your overall comprehension skills.
    • \n\t
  • When facing questions that require rearranging sentences or paragraphs to form a coherent sequence, consider the following steps:\n\t
      \n\t\t
    • Read Carefully: Go through each sentence attentively to understand its context, topic, and the information it provides. Note any connecting phrases or words that link sentences together.
      • \n\t\t
    • Identify the Logical Flow: Look for clues within the sentences that indicate a sequence. Sentences might introduce a topic, provide details, give examples, or conclude the idea. Pay attention to time markers, cause-and-effect relationships, or other transitional phrases.
      • \n\t\t
    • Start with Key Points: Sometimes, certain sentences act as introductory or concluding statements. Begin with sentences that introduce the topic or provide a conclusion, as they often set the context or summarize the content.
      • \n\t\t
    • Connect Related Ideas: Identify relationships between sentences. For example, a sentence might explain or support the idea presented in the previous sentence. Look for coherence and progression in the ideas presented.
      • \n\t\t
    • Consider Keyword Consistency: Check for keywords or recurring themes that link sentences. These can help establish a logical sequence. Pay attention to pronouns (he, it, they) referring to previously mentioned subjects.
      • \n\t\t
    • Evaluate Options: Once you have a tentative sequence, review it to ensure that the ideas flow logically and coherently. Eliminate options that disrupt the logical flow or create inconsistencies.
      • \n\t\t
    • Test the Flow: Mentally or visually run through the proposed sequence to verify if it reads smoothly and makes sense. Ensure that there's a clear progression from the beginning to the end.
      • \n\t\t
    • Double-Check: Before finalizing your answer, double-check the sequence to confirm that it presents a cohesive and logical progression of ideas. 
      • \n\t
    \n\t
    • \n

      \n\t\t

      • \n\t\t
    • took through: This phrase doesn't commonly exist as a phrasal verb. "Take through" might mean guiding or leading someone through a process, but it doesn’t align with the context provided.
      • \n\t\t

Identify the sentence that provides the meaning of the given idiom.

\n\n

Under the cloud 

\n", "comp": "", "opts": ["The classmates were dancing together under the rain.", "The driver was under suspicion after the police found blood on his car. ", "The Red fort looks beautiful amidst the cloudy weather and cold atmosphere. ", "It was the best place to sit under the sky with hands full of food."], "cidx": 1, "sol": "

The correct answer is 'The driver was under suspicion after the police found blood on his car.'

\n\n

Key Points

\n\n
    \n\t
  • The sentence that provides the meaning of the idiom "under the cloud" is: 2) The driver was under suspicion after the police found blood on his car.
    • \n\t
  • Explanation: The idiom "under the cloud" typically refers to being in a situation of trouble, disgrace, or suspicion. In the provided sentence, the driver being "under suspicion" aligns with the meaning of the idiom, indicating a negative or questionable situation.
    \n\t 
    • \n
\n\n

Correct Answer: The driver was under suspicion after the police found blood on his car. 

\n\n

Additional Information

\n\n
    \n\t
  • Origin: The origin of the idiom "under the cloud" is believed to date back centuries and likely comes from the association of clouds with uncertainty or trouble. Being "under a cloud" signifies being in a shadow or obscured by something negative, much like how a literal cloud can cast a shadow on the ground. Over time, it has been used figuratively to imply being in an unfavorable or problematic situation.

\n\n

Key Points

\n\n
    \n\t
  • The given sentence is in the active voice. As per the question we have to change it into passive voice. 
    • \n\t
  • The process of transformation is as follows:\n\t
      \n\t\t
    • We know that 'Who' is changed into 'By whom' in 'active to passive' voice transformation. 
      • \n\t\t
    • The structure of it as follows: \n\t\t
        \n\t\t\t
      • Who+has+V3+object?
        • \n\t\t\t
      • By whom+has+Object+been+V3?
        • \n\t\t
      \n\t\t
      • \n\t\t
    • 'teaches' will be changed into 'is taught'. 
      • \n\t\t
    • Example:\n\t\t
        \n\t\t\t
      • Who has broken the chair? (active voice)
        • \n\t\t\t
      • By whom has the chair been broken? (passive voice)
        • \n\t\t
      \n\t\t
      • \n\t
    \n\t
    • \n\t
  • Therefore, as per the points mentioned above, we find that the correct answer is Option 3. 
    \n\t​

\n\n

Correct Sentence: By whom is she taught geography?

The correct answer is 'DECAB'.

Key Points

    \n\t
  • The given question is an example of a Para Jumble.
    • \n\t
  • We know that the first statement of a para jumbled question is usually an independent general statement, a noun, a universal fact, starting of an incident, or it starts with 'most' or 'once'.
    • \n\t
  • The correct sequence to form a coherent paragraph is: DECAB.\n\t
      \n\t\t
    • D. The Dead Sea is one of the saltiest bodies of water on the planet, with about ten times the salt content of typical saltwater.
      • \n\t\t

      • \n\t\t

    • \n\t

Correct Sequence: Posters are used to promote a brand or pass on important information. They can be used for varied purposes ranging from telling people to be cautious as the floor is wet, to informing them about an upcoming sale or event. They usually include a picture and very few words. A suitable caption makes a poster effective and memorable.

\n\n
Additional Information
\n\n
    \n\t
  • Anticipation is a feeling of excitement about something that is going to happen in the near future. In the context of a poster's elements, anticipation is more of an emotional response rather than a tangible component of a poster.
    • \n\t
  • Quotation refers to a group of words taken from a text or speech and repeated by someone other than the original author or speaker. Although a quotation can be used in a poster, it is not as universally applicable or necessary for effectiveness as a caption.
    • \n\t
  • Caution is a warning against danger or risk. While caution could be the focus of a poster's message, especially in safety posters, it is not an element that inherently makes a poster effective or memorable across all contexts.

लाला लाजपत राय:

\n\n
    \n\t
  • लाला लाजपत राय ने देश को आजादी दिलाने में बहुत बड़ा योगदान दिया।
    • \n\t
  • एक महान स्वतंत्रता सेनानी और नेता के अलावा, लाला लाजपत राय एक प्रसिद्ध लेखक भी थे।
    • \n\t
  • साइमन कमीशन के आगमन का विरोध कर रहे कार्यकर्ताओं पर पुलिस द्वारा लाठीचार्ज किये जाने से उनकी मृत्यु हो गयी।
    • \n\t
  • 30 अक्टूबर, 1928 को साइमन कमीशन के सदस्यों के लाहौर आगमन का बहिष्कार करने के लिए लाजपत राय की अध्यक्षता में एक शांतिपूर्ण जुलूस निकाला गया।
    • \n\t
  • मार्च को रोकते हुए, पुलिस अधीक्षक, स्कॉट ने अपने पुलिस बल को 'कार्यकर्ताओं पर लाठीचार्ज' करने का आदेश दिया।
    • \n\t

    • \n\t
  • लेकिन, क्रांतिकारियों ने सहायक पुलिस अधीक्षक जे.पी. सॉन्डर्स को स्कॉट समझकर उसकी हत्या कर दी।

Additional Information
\n  चितरंजन दास

\n\n
    \n\t
  • उनका जन्म 5 नवंबर 1869 को ढाका, बांग्लादेश में हुआ था।
    • \n\t
  • उन्हें लोकप्रिय रूप से देशबंधु कहा जाता था जिसका अर्थ है देश का मित्र।
    • \n\t
  • वह एक भारतीय राजनीतिज्ञ और ब्रिटिश शासन के तहत बंगाल में स्वराज (स्वतंत्रता) पार्टी के नेता थे।
    • \n\t
  • 1919-1922 के असहयोग आंदोलन के दौरान वह बंगाल में एक अग्रणी व्यक्ति थे।
    • \n\t
  • उन्होंने ब्रिटिश कपड़ों पर प्रतिबंध लगाने की पहल की और अपने यूरोपीय कपड़े जलाकर और खादी कपड़े पहनकर एक उदाहरण स्थापित किया।
    • \n\t
  • उन्होंने अलीपुर बम मामले में अरबिंदो घोष का सफलतापूर्वक बचाव किया।
    • \n\t
  • उन्होंने ब्रिटिश राज से लड़ने के लिए 'फॉरवर्ड' नाम से एक अखबार निकाला और बाद में इसका नाम बदलकर 'लिबर्टी' रख दिया।
    • \n

\n\n
    \n\t

    • \n\t
  • काकोरी षडयंत्र में शामिल होने के कारण सान्याल को भी जेल हुई लेकिन बाद में उन्हें रिहा कर दिया गया।
    • \n\t
  • सचिन्द्र नाथ चन्द्रशेखर आज़ाद और भगत सिंह जैसे क्रांतिकारियों के गुरु भी थे।
    • \n\t
  • जेल में उन्हें तपेदिक हो गया और उनके अंतिम महीनों के लिए उन्हें गोरखपुर जेल भेज दिया गया।

  • गलियारा आमतौर पर उत्तर में श्रीनगर से शुरू होता है और दक्षिण में कन्याकुमारी तक फैला होता है।
    • \n\t
  • उत्तर-दक्षिण गलियारा का प्राथमिक उद्देश्य संयोजकता को बढ़ाना, यात्रा के समय को कम करना और विभिन्न क्षेत्रों के बीच वस्तुओं और लोगों की सुचारू आवाजाही को सुविधाजनक बनाना है।
    • \n

\n\n

\n\n

लाभ% = 5/125 × 100

\n\n

= 4%

\n\n

∴ उत्तर 4% है।

दिया गया है:

\n\n

क्रमिक छूटें 5%, 10% और p% हैं। 

\n\n

एकल छूट = 31.6%

\n\n

संकल्पना: यदि MP पर a%, b% और c% की तीन क्रमिक छूटें हैं। 

\n\n

SP = MP(100 - a)/100  × (100 - b)/100 × (100 - c)/100 

\n\n

प्रयुक्त सूत्र:

\n\n

SP = MP × (100 - D%)/100

\n\n

D% = (D/MP) × 100

\n\n

गणना:

\n\n

माना MP, x है। 

\n\n

SP = x × (100 - 5)/100 × (100 - 10)/100 × (100 - p)/100

\n\n

⇒ SP = x × 95/100 × 90/100 × (100 - p)/100

\n\n

⇒ x × 95/100 × 90/100 × (100 - p)/100 =  x × (100 - 31.6)/100 

\n\n

⇒ x × 19/20 × 9/10 × (100 - p)/100 =  x × 68.4/100 

\n\n

\n\n

\n\n

⇒ (100 - p) = 80

\n\n

⇒ p = 20

\n\n

Important Points

\n\n
    \n\t
  • Practice is key! Regularly practicing rearranging sentences or paragraphs will improve your ability to discern logical sequences and enhance your overall comprehension skills.
    • \n\t
  • When facing questions that require rearranging sentences or paragraphs to form a coherent sequence, consider the following steps:\n\t
      \n\t\t
    • Read Carefully: Go through each sentence attentively to understand its context, topic, and the information it provides. Note any connecting phrases or words that link sentences together.
      • \n\t\t
    • Identify the Logical Flow: Look for clues within the sentences that indicate a sequence. Sentences might introduce a topic, provide details, give examples, or conclude the idea. Pay attention to time markers, cause-and-effect relationships, or other transitional phrases.
      • \n\t\t
    • Start with Key Points: Sometimes, certain sentences act as introductory or concluding statements. Begin with sentences that introduce the topic or provide a conclusion, as they often set the context or summarize the content.
      • \n\t\t
    • Connect Related Ideas: Identify relationships between sentences. For example, a sentence might explain or support the idea presented in the previous sentence. Look for coherence and progression in the ideas presented.
      • \n\t\t
    • Consider Keyword Consistency: Check for keywords or recurring themes that link sentences. These can help establish a logical sequence. Pay attention to pronouns (he, it, they) referring to previously mentioned subjects.
      • \n\t\t
    • Evaluate Options: Once you have a tentative sequence, review it to ensure that the ideas flow logically and coherently. Eliminate options that disrupt the logical flow or create inconsistencies.
      • \n\t\t
    • Test the Flow: Mentally or visually run through the proposed sequence to verify if it reads smoothly and makes sense. Ensure that there's a clear progression from the beginning to the end.
      • \n\t\t
    • Double-Check: Before finalizing your answer, double-check the sequence to confirm that it presents a cohesive and logical progression of ideas. 
      • \n\t
    \n\t

Which of the following can be considered as an example of flow variable?

\n\n

(i) Production of rice

\n\n

(ii) Import of cloth

\n\n

(iii) Change in capital 

\n", "comp": "", "opts": ["None of (i), (ii) and (iii)", "Only (iii)", "All of (i), (ii) and (iii)", "Only (i) and (ii)"], "cidx": 2, "sol": "

The correct answer is All of (i), (ii) and (iii).Key Points

\n\n
    \n\t
  • A flow is a quantity that is measured with reference to a period of time.
    • \n\t
  • Thus, flows are defined with reference to a specific period (length of time).
    • \n\t
  • National income is a flow.
    • \n\t
  • It describes and measures flow of goods and services which become available to a country during a year.
    • \n\t
  •  Example of flow variable are Production of rice, Import of cloth and Change in capital.
    • \n
\n\n

Additional Information Stock Variable

\n\n
    \n\t
  • Stocks are defined at a particular point in time.\n\n\t
      \n\t\t
    • So, Wealth is a stock variable as it can be measured at a particular time. 
      • \n\t
    \n\t
    • \n\t
  • A flow shows change during a period of time whereas a stock indicates the quantity of a variable at a point of time.
    • \n\t
  • Wealth is a stock since it can be measured at a point of time.
    • \n\t
  • Income is a flow because it can be measured over a period of time.
    • \n\t
  • Salary and change in wealth is a flow because it can be measured over a period of time.
    • \n

    તે મહાભારત અને રામાયણ, સ્થાનિક લોકકથાઓ અને અમૂર્ત વિષયો સહિત મહાકાવ્યોના ગદ્યાન્શને રજૂ કરે છે.

    \n\t
    • \n\t
  • \n\t

    એક સદીથી વધુ જૂના, આ નૃત્ય સ્વરૂપને રાજવી પરિવારોના આશ્રયદાતાઓ, સમૃદ્ધ જમીનદારો અને પ્રદેશના બ્રિટિશ ગવર્નરો દ્વારા વ્યાપકપણે સમર્થન આપવામાં આવ્યું હતું.

    \n\t
    • \n\t
  • \n\t

    આ પ્રદર્શન મુખ્યત્વે ગજન ઉત્સવ દરમિયાન મૂકવામાં આવે છે, જે ભગવાન શિવને માન આપવા માટે થાય છે.
    \n\t

Select the correct option about Kwashiorkor.

\n\n

Statement A: It is a form of malnutrition caused by a lack of fat in the diet.

\n\n

Statement B: Children who develop Kwashiorkor may not grow or develop properly and may remain stunted for the rest of their lives.

\n", "comp": "", "opts": ["Only statement B is correct", "Both statements A and B are correct", "Both statements A and B are incorrect", "Only statement A is correct"], "cidx": 0, "sol": "

The correct answer is ​Option 1) Only statement B is correct.

\n\n

Key Points

\n\n
    \n\t
  •  Lack of protein in the diet causes Kwashiorkor, so the statement A is incorrect .
    • \n\t
  • Protein is found in every cell of the human body
    • \n\t
  • Kwashiorkor is a severe form of malnutrition that is most prevalent in children, resulting primarily from protein deficiency.
    • \n\t
  • Children who develop Kwashiorkor may not grow or develop properly and may remain stunted for the rest of their lives, so statement B is correct.
    • \n\t
  • This condition, also referred to as "edematous malnutrition," is often characterized by swelling in different parts of the body, such as the ankles, feet, and belly, giving the patient a thin appearance.
    • \n\t
  • This disease is more common in rural areas, particularly in the sub-Saharan regions.
    • \n\t
  • Areas that have experienced famine or have a limited supply of food are more susceptible to Kwashiorkor.
    • \n\t
  • Furthermore, regions where the knowledge of a proper and balanced diet is lacking also see a high number of Kwashiorkor cases.
    • \n
\n", "pos": 0, "neg": 0}, {"n": 38, "si": 1, "text": "In Which year did John Newlands propound the 'Law of Octaves' an innovative concept proposing the periodicity of chemical elements arranged in the order of atomic weight?", "comp": "", "opts": ["1861", "1863", "1865", "1867"], "cidx": 2, "sol": "

The correct answer is 1865.

\n\n

Key Points

\n\n
    \n\t
  • The 'Law of Octaves' was propounded by the English chemist J.A.R. Newlands in 1865.
    • \n\t
  • The 'Law of Octaves'states that if the chemical elements are arranged according to increasing atomic weight, those with similar physical and chemical properties occur after each interval of seven elements.
    • \n\t
  • J.A.R. Newlands was the first to disclose a periodic pattern in the properties of the elements and anticipated later developments of the periodic law.​
    • \n
\n\n

Additional Information

\n\n

There were the following limitations of the Newland's Law of Octaves

\n\n
    \n\t
  • It was found that the Law of Octaves was applicable only up to calcium, as after calcium every eighth element did not possess properties similar to that of the first.
    • \n\t
  • It was assumed by Newlands that only 56 elements existed in nature and no more elements would be discovered in the future. But, later on, several new elements were discovered, whose properties did not fit into the Law of Octaves.
    • \n\t
  • After the discovery of the Noble gases, the Law of Octaves became irrelevant. Thus, Newlands’ Law of Octaves worked well with lighter elements only.
    • \n
\n", "pos": 0, "neg": 0}, {"n": 39, "si": 1, "text": "ભારતીય બંધારણનો કયો ભાગ મૂળભૂત અધિકારો સાથે સંબંધિત છે?", "comp": "", "opts": ["ભાગ IX", "ભાગ VI", "ભાગ XI", "ભાગ III"], "cidx": 3, "sol": "

સાચો જવાબ ભાગ III છે.

    • \n\t
  • મૂળભૂત અધિકારોના ઉલ્લંઘનના કિસ્સામાં, ભારતના નાગરિક SC (સુપ્રીમ કોર્ટ) અથવા HC (હાઈ કોર્ટ)માં જઈ શકે છે.
    • \n\t

The correct answer is Kathak.

\n\n

Key Points

\n\n
    \n\t
  • Kathak dancer and choreographer Aditi Mangaldas was born in 1960.
    • \n\t
  • She is renowned for her repertoire in both classical Kathak and "contemporary dance based on kathak."
    • \n\t
  • Mangaldas is regarded as one of the best Kathak dancers in the world, both in India and outside.
    • \n\t
  • She has won numerous awards for her notable contributions in Kathak including the National Sangeet Natak Akademi Award in 2013. 
    • \n
\n\n

Additional Information

\n\n

Kathak:

\n\n
    \n\t
  • The well-known ancient Indian classical dance known as Kathak is said to have originated with the nomadic storytellers from North India known as Kathakars.
    • \n\t
  • These Kathakars traveled and told tales using songs, dances, and music that were reminiscent of classical Greek theatre.
    • \n\t
  • The foundations of Kathak are rooted in Natya Shastra, an ancient Sanskrit text written by Bharata Muni.
    • \n\t
  • The three chief sections of a Kathak dance are:\n\t
      \n\t\t
    • Invocation: The act of an artist offering a prayer or salutation to God and their guru is known as an invocation.\n\t\t
        \n\t\t\t
      • Hindu performers employ mudras, or hand gestures, to convey the same message.
        • \n\t\t\t
      • On Muslim holidays, the artist presents a "salami."
        • \n\t\t
      \n\t\t
      • \n\t\t
    • Nritta: The artist's portrayal of pure dancing.\n\t\t
        \n\t\t\t
      • He or she begins by moving the neck, wrists, and eyebrows slowly and elegantly.
        • \n\t\t\t
      • The quick sequences according to "bols" come next. A bol is a brief rhythmic pattern sequence. Here, the artist also demonstrates lively footwork.
        • \n\t\t
      \n\t\t
      • \n\t\t
    • Nritya: In this piece, the performer uses facial expressions, slow body motions, and vocal and instrumental music to tell a tale or explore a theme.
      • \n\t
    \n\t
    • \n\t
  • 8 important Classical Dance forms of India:
    • \n

\n\n

Key Points

\n\n
    \n\t
  • The North-South Corridor is one of the key components of the National Highways Development Project (NHDP).
    • \n\t
  • It involves the development of a high-quality road network that connects the northern and southern parts of India.
    • \n\t
  • The corridor typically starts from Srinagar in the north and extends down to Kanyakumari in the south.
    • \n\t
  • The primary objective of the North-South Corridor is to enhance connectivity, reduce travel time, and facilitate the smooth movement of goods and people between different regions.
    • \n
\n\n

Additional Information

\n\n
    \n\t
  • The East-West Corridor is a part of NHDP Phase-II. It involves the development of a high-quality road network connecting the eastern and western regions of India.\n\n\t
      \n\t\t
    • The corridor typically spans from Porbandar in Gujarat on the western coast to Silchar in Assam on the eastern border.
      • \n\t
    \n\t
    • \n\t
  • The Golden Quadrilateral (GQ) is a significant highway network in India and one of the key components of the National Highways Development Project (NHDP). \n\t
      \n\t\t
    • The primary objective of the Golden Quadrilateral project is to create a high-quality road network linking the four major metropolitan cities of India, forming a quadrilateral shape.
      • \n\t\t
    • The cities included in the quadrilateral are Delhi (North), Mumbai (West), Chennai (South), and Kolkata (East).
      • \n\t\t
    • The first phase, covering the main routes connecting the four cities, was initiated in 2001.
      • \n\t
    \n\t
    • \n

The correct answer is Due to the South-West monsoon.

\n\n

Key Points

\n\n
    \n\t
  • The South-west monsoon is the primary rainy season in India, which starts in June and lasts till September.\n\n\t
      \n\t\t
    • This monsoon is caused by the differential heating of land and sea, which results in the formation of low-pressure systems over the Indian Ocean and the Arabian Sea.​​
      • \n\t
    \n\t
    • \n
\n\n

Additional Information

\n\n
    \n\t
  • Western Disturbances are low-pressure systems that originate in the Mediterranean region and move towards the Indian subcontinent.\n\n\t
      \n\t\t
    • These disturbances bring rainfall to the north-western parts of India during the winter months, and they also affect the weather conditions in other parts of the country.
      • \n\t
    \n\t
    • \n\t
  • The North-east is known as the winter monsoon, the northeast monsoon approaches India from the northeast.\n\t
      \n\t\t
    • During this time, rainfall is common throughout the southern region of India, particularly in Kerala, Andhra Pradesh, Karnataka, and Tamil Nadu.
      • \n\t
    \n\t
    • \n\t
  • The Retreating Monsoon, also known as the North-East monsoon, brings rainfall to the southern parts of India during October and November.\n\t
      \n\t\t
    • This monsoon is caused by the withdrawal of the south-west monsoon winds and the formation of a low-pressure system over the Bay of Bengal.
      • \n\t
    \n\t
    • \n

    • Example: a moment of irresolution...

      • \n\t
    \n\t
    • \n

Important Points

\n\n
    \n\t
  • Practice is key! Regularly practicing rearranging sentences or paragraphs will improve your ability to discern logical sequences and enhance your overall comprehension skills.
    • \n\t
  • When facing questions that require rearranging sentences or paragraphs to form a coherent sequence, consider the following steps:\n\t
      \n\t\t
    • Read Carefully: Go through each sentence attentively to understand its context, topic, and the information it provides. Note any connecting phrases or words that link sentences together.
      • \n\t\t
    • Identify the Logical Flow: Look for clues within the sentences that indicate a sequence. Sentences might introduce a topic, provide details, give examples, or conclude the idea. Pay attention to time markers, cause-and-effect relationships, or other transitional phrases.
      • \n\t\t
    • Start with Key Points: Sometimes, certain sentences act as introductory or concluding statements. Begin with sentences that introduce the topic or provide a conclusion, as they often set the context or summarize the content.
      • \n\t\t
    • Connect Related Ideas: Identify relationships between sentences. For example, a sentence might explain or support the idea presented in the previous sentence. Look for coherence and progression in the ideas presented.
      • \n\t\t
    • Consider Keyword Consistency: Check for keywords or recurring themes that link sentences. These can help establish a logical sequence. Pay attention to pronouns (he, it, they) referring to previously mentioned subjects.
      • \n\t\t
    • Evaluate Options: Once you have a tentative sequence, review it to ensure that the ideas flow logically and coherently. Eliminate options that disrupt the logical flow or create inconsistencies.
      • \n\t\t
    • Test the Flow: Mentally or visually run through the proposed sequence to verify if it reads smoothly and makes sense. Ensure that there's a clear progression from the beginning to the end.
      • \n\t\t
    • Double-Check: Before finalizing your answer, double-check the sequence to confirm that it presents a cohesive and logical progression of ideas. 
      • \n\t
    \n\t

'; } // ══════════════════════════════════════════ // NAVIGATION // ══════════════════════════════════════════ function goToQ(n) { // save time for previous question if(curQ && curMode === 'attempt') { qTimes[String(curQ)] += Math.round((Date.now() - qStartTime) / 1000); qVisited[String(curQ)] = true; // update per-question timer display var ts = qTimes[String(curQ)]; var el = document.getElementById('qt' + curQ); if(el) el.textContent = (ts >= 60 ? Math.floor(ts/60)+':'+pad(ts%60) : '00:'+pad(ts)); } // hide previous card var prevCard = document.getElementById('acard' + curQ); if(prevCard) prevCard.classList.remove('active-card'); qStartTime = Date.now(); curQ = n; qVisited[String(n)] = true; var q = Q_DATA[n-1]; curSec = q.si; // update sec tabs document.querySelectorAll('.stab').forEach(function(b,i){ b.classList.toggle('active', i === curSec); }); // show current card only var card = document.getElementById('acard' + n); if(card) { card.classList.add('active-card'); window.scrollTo(0, 0); } refreshPalette(); } function secTabClick(si) { curSec = si; document.querySelectorAll('.stab').forEach(function(b,i){ b.classList.toggle('active', i===si); }); // go to first q of this section if(SEC_QNUMS[si] && SEC_QNUMS[si].length) goToQ(SEC_QNUMS[si][0]); } function saveAndNext() { // just advance nextQ(); } function markAndNext() { marked[String(curQ)] = true; refreshPalette(); updateQCircle(curQ); nextQ(); } function clearCurrent() { answers[String(curQ)] = -1; marked[String(curQ)] = false; renderOpts(curQ); updateQCircle(curQ); refreshPalette(); } function nextQ() { if(curQ < TOTAL) goToQ(curQ + 1); } function prevQ() { if(curQ > 1) goToQ(curQ - 1); } // ══════════════════════════════════════════ // OPTION SELECT // ══════════════════════════════════════════ function selectOpt(qn, oi) { if(submitted && !reattempt) return; if(submitted && reattempt) { // reattempt mode: allow re-selection answers[String(qn)] = oi; renderOpts(qn); return; } // toggle if(answers[String(qn)] === oi) { answers[String(qn)] = -1; } else { answers[String(qn)] = oi; } renderOpts(qn); updateQCircle(qn); refreshPalette(); } function renderOpts(qn) { var q = Q_DATA[qn-1]; q.opts.forEach(function(_, oi) { var el = document.getElementById('opta_' + qn + '_' + oi); if(!el) return; el.classList.remove('sel','reveal-correct','reveal-wrong'); var icon = document.getElementById('oicon_' + qn + '_' + oi); if(icon) icon.innerHTML = ''; if(submitted && !reattempt) { if(oi === q.cidx) { el.classList.add('reveal-correct'); if(icon) icon.innerHTML = '✓'; } else if(oi === answers[String(qn)] && answers[String(qn)] !== q.cidx) { el.classList.add('reveal-wrong'); if(icon) icon.innerHTML = '✗ ○ ↺'; } } else if(answers[String(qn)] === oi) { el.classList.add('sel'); } }); } // ══════════════════════════════════════════ // Q CIRCLE / PALETTE // ══════════════════════════════════════════ function stateClass(qn) { if(submitted) { var ans = answers[String(qn)]; var correct = Q_MAP[String(qn)].correct; if(ans === -1) return ''; return ans === correct ? 'correct' : 'wrong'; } if(marked[String(qn)]) return 'marked'; if(answers[String(qn)] !== -1) return 'attempted'; return ''; } function updateQCircle(qn) { var el = document.getElementById('qnc' + qn); if(!el) return; el.className = 'qnum-circle ' + stateClass(qn); } function refreshPalette() { // update palette buttons Q_DATA.forEach(function(q) { var btn = document.getElementById('palbtn' + q.n); if(!btn) return; btn.className = 'pal-btn'; var sc = stateClass(q.n); if(sc === 'attempted') btn.classList.add('p-attempted'); else if(sc === 'marked') btn.classList.add('p-marked'); else if(sc === 'correct') btn.classList.add('p-correct'); else if(sc === 'wrong') btn.classList.add('p-wrong'); if(q.n === curQ) btn.classList.add('p-current'); }); // update section stats SEC_NAMES.forEach(function(_, si) { var qns = SEC_QNUMS[si]; var att=0, mrk=0, unatt=0, unseen=0; qns.forEach(function(qn) { if(marked[String(qn)]) mrk++; else if(answers[String(qn)] !== -1) att++; else if(!qVisited[String(qn)]) unseen++; else unatt++; }); var el = document.getElementById('spstats' + si); if(el) el.innerHTML = '
★ '+mrk+'
' + '
'+att+'
' + '
'+unatt+'
' + '
'+unseen+'
'; }); // update q circles for all Q_DATA.forEach(function(q) { updateQCircle(q.n); }); } function toggleSpSec(si) { var pal = document.getElementById('sppal'+si); var arrow = document.getElementById('sp-arrow'+si); if(!pal) return; if(pal.style.display === 'none') { pal.style.display = ''; if(arrow) arrow.textContent = '∧'; } else { pal.style.display = 'none'; if(arrow) arrow.textContent = '∨'; } } // ══════════════════════════════════════════ // BOOKMARK / STAR // ══════════════════════════════════════════ function toggleBookmark(qn, e) { e.stopPropagation(); var btn = document.getElementById('qibookmark'+qn); if(!btn) return; btn.classList.toggle('bookmark-on'); } function toggleStar(qn, e) { e.stopPropagation(); marked[String(qn)] = !marked[String(qn)]; var btn = document.getElementById('qistar'+qn); if(btn) { btn.classList.toggle('star-on', marked[String(qn)]); btn.textContent = marked[String(qn)] ? '★' : '☆'; } updateQCircle(qn); refreshPalette(); } // ══════════════════════════════════════════ // PANEL // ══════════════════════════════════════════ function openPanel() { document.getElementById('side-overlay').style.display = 'block'; document.getElementById('side-panel').classList.add('open'); refreshPalette(); } function closePanel() { document.getElementById('side-overlay').style.display = 'none'; document.getElementById('side-panel').classList.remove('open'); } function spTab(i) { document.querySelectorAll('.sp-tab').forEach(function(b,idx){ b.classList.toggle('active',idx===i); }); } // ══════════════════════════════════════════ // SUBMIT // ══════════════════════════════════════════ function openSubmitModal() { var att=0, mrk=0; Object.keys(answers).forEach(function(k){ if(answers[k]!==-1) att++; if(marked[k]) mrk++; }); document.getElementById('modal-attempted').textContent = att; document.getElementById('modal-unattempted').textContent = TOTAL - att; document.getElementById('modal-marked').textContent = mrk; document.getElementById('modal-timer').textContent = document.getElementById('hdr-timer').textContent; document.getElementById('submit-modal').classList.add('show'); closePanel(); } function closeSubmitModal() { document.getElementById('submit-modal').classList.remove('show'); } function confirmSubmit() { closeSubmitModal(); submitted = true; clearInterval(timerInterval); // reveal all opts in attempt mode Q_DATA.forEach(function(q) { renderOpts(q.n); }); refreshPalette(); // show analysis page showAnalysis(); } function normCdf(z) { // Abramowitz-Stegun approximation of the standard normal CDF. var t = 1/(1+0.2316419*Math.abs(z)); var d = 0.3989423*Math.exp(-z*z/2); var p = d*t*(0.3193815+t*(-0.3565638+t*(1.781478+t*(-1.821256+t*1.330274)))); return z>0 ? 1-p : p; } function renderRank(score, maxScore) { var card = document.getElementById('ana-rank-card'); var A = ANALYSIS || {}; var attempts = A.attempts || 0; var maxMarks = A.maxMarks || maxScore || 0; var topper = A.topperMarks || 0; var avg = A.avgMarks; // Nothing useful to estimate from → hide the card entirely. if(!attempts && (avg===undefined||avg===null) && !topper) { card.style.display='none'; return; } // Approximate the score distribution as a normal curve. if(avg===undefined||avg===null) avg = topper ? topper*0.45 : maxMarks*0.35; var std = (topper && topper>avg) ? (topper-avg)/3 : Math.max(1,(maxMarks||1)*0.15); if(std<=0) std = Math.max(1,(maxMarks||1)*0.15); var z = (score-avg)/std; var pct = normCdf(z)*100; if(topper && score>=topper) pct = 99.9; pct = Math.max(0.1, Math.min(99.9, pct)); document.getElementById('ana-percentile').textContent = pct.toFixed(1) + ' %ile'; if(attempts) { var rank = Math.max(1, Math.round((1-pct/100)*attempts)); if(topper && score>=topper) rank = 1; document.getElementById('ana-rank').textContent = '#' + rank.toLocaleString(); document.getElementById('ana-attempts').textContent = attempts.toLocaleString(); } else { document.getElementById('ana-rank').textContent = '-'; document.getElementById('ana-attempts').textContent = '-'; } var meta = []; if(topper) meta.push('Topper: ' + (topper%1===0?topper:topper.toFixed(1)) + ' / ' + maxMarks); if(A.avgMarks!==undefined && A.avgMarks!==null) meta.push('Avg: ' + (A.avgMarks%1===0?A.avgMarks:A.avgMarks.toFixed(1))); if(A.cutoff) meta.push('Cutoff: ' + A.cutoff); meta.push('Estimated from Testbook attempt data — actual rank may vary.'); document.getElementById('ana-rank-meta').innerHTML = meta.join('  •  '); card.style.display = 'block'; } function showAnalysis() { var totalCorrect=0, totalWrong=0, totalSkip=0; var totalScore=0, maxScore=0; Q_DATA.forEach(function(q) { var ans = answers[String(q.n)]; var pos = Q_MAP[String(q.n)].pos || 1; var neg = Q_MAP[String(q.n)].neg || 0; maxScore += pos; if(ans === -1) { totalSkip++; } else if(ans === q.cidx) { totalCorrect++; totalScore += pos; } else { totalWrong++; totalScore -= neg; } }); var attempted = totalCorrect + totalWrong; var accuracy = attempted > 0 ? Math.round((totalCorrect / attempted) * 100) : 0; document.getElementById('ana-score').textContent = totalScore % 1 === 0 ? totalScore : totalScore.toFixed(2); document.getElementById('ana-max').textContent = maxScore % 1 === 0 ? maxScore : maxScore.toFixed(2); document.getElementById('ana-accuracy').textContent = accuracy + ' %'; document.getElementById('ana-attempted').textContent = attempted + '/' + TOTAL; document.getElementById('ana-correct-count').textContent = totalCorrect; document.getElementById('ana-wrong-count').textContent = totalWrong; // Estimated rank & percentile (only when Testbook attempt stats exist) renderRank(totalScore, maxScore); // Question analysis grid document.getElementById('ana-grid').innerHTML = '
' + totalCorrect + '
Correct
' + '
' + totalWrong + '
Incorrect
' + '
' + totalSkip + '
Unattempted
' + '
' + TOTAL + '
Total Questions
'; // Section-wise breakdown var secHtml = ''; SEC_NAMES.forEach(function(name, si) { var qns = SEC_QNUMS[si]; var sc=0, wc=0, sk=0, secScore=0, secMax=0; qns.forEach(function(qn) { var ans = answers[String(qn)]; var pos = Q_MAP[String(qn)].pos || 1; var neg = Q_MAP[String(qn)].neg || 0; secMax += pos; if(ans === -1) sk++; else if(ans === Q_MAP[String(qn)].correct) { sc++; secScore += pos; } else { wc++; secScore -= neg; } }); var pct = secMax > 0 ? Math.round(Math.max(0, secScore) / secMax * 100) : 0; secHtml += '
' + '' + name + '' + '
' + '' + (secScore % 1 === 0 ? secScore : secScore.toFixed(1)) + '/' + secMax + '' + '
'; }); document.getElementById('ana-sections').innerHTML = secHtml; switchMode('analysis'); } // ══════════════════════════════════════════ // MODE SWITCH // ══════════════════════════════════════════ function switchMode(mode) { curMode = mode; var isAttempt = mode === 'attempt'; var isView = mode === 'view'; var isAnalysis = mode === 'analysis'; document.getElementById('hdr-attempt').style.display = isAttempt ? }); Q_DATA.forEach(function(q) { var card = document.getElementById('vcard' + q.n); var qbtn = document.getElementById('sqbtn' + q.n); var show = (si === -1 || q.si === si); if(card) card.style.display = show ? 'flex' : 'none'; }); } // ══════════════════════════════════════════ // REATTEMPT TOGGLE // ══════════════════════════════════════════ function toggleReattempt() { reattempt = !reattempt; var track = document.getElementById('rt-track'); var thumb = document.getElementById('rt-thumb'); track.classList.toggle('off', !reattempt); thumb.classList.toggle('on', reattempt); } // ══════════════════════════════════════════ // TIMER // ══════════════════════════════════════════ function startTimer() { timerInterval = setInterval(function() { if(paused) return; timerSecs--; if(timerSecs <= 0) { timerSecs = 0; clearInterval(timerInterval); confirmSubmit(); } var h = Math.floor(timerSecs/3600); var m = Math.floor((timerSecs%3600)/60); var s = timerSecs%60; var str = pad(h)+':'+pad(m)+':'+pad(s); document.getElementById('hdr-timer').textContent = str; document.getElementById('modal-timer').textContent = str; }, 1000); } function pad(n){ return n<10?'0'+n:n; } function togglePause() { paused = !paused; document.getElementById('btn-pause').textContent = paused ? 'Image failed to load' : 'Offline - image unavailable'; } }, true); // ══════════════════════════════════════════ // INIT // ══════════════════════════════════════════ buildAll();
